# Scenario catalog — mail-archive-tool

Per assurance-kit `process/scenario-catalog.md`. The tables here are the machine
contract the covers-map meta-test (`assureblock/covers_map_test.go`) parses — do
not restyle them. The tests encode the invariants; a change that cannot satisfy
an invariant is the thing that is wrong.

## 1. ID prefix

`MA-` (test-spec IDs). Invariants use the `R` series.

## 2. Invariants (what must always hold)

- **R1 — No silent loss.** Every message the reader yields is exported, and no
  attachment/inline image is silently dropped: content referenced but absent
  (not downloaded, unparseable, torn) is recorded durably in the manifest —
  classed *fillable* (an on-demand source may still deliver it) or *terminal*
  (a complete-at-fetch source never will) — and listed in the verification
  report regenerated from the manifest on every run, never hidden. Incremental
  runs revisit every fillable gap and fill it once the source holds the content.
- **R2 — Incremental idempotence.** An incremental re-run over an unchanged
  source exports zero new items; `full` re-exports all. The manifest is the sole
  dedup authority.
- **R3 — Stable identity; one copy per mailbox on the live path.** A message's
  dedup key is stable across runs (Internet Message-ID when present,
  deterministic content hash otherwise). A message captured from a LIVE source
  (Graph; IMAP later) is stored once per mailbox, keyed by identity: its folder
  over time is recorded (in the record's Folder/FirstFolder and the history log),
  and the current/served view groups it under its current — or a chosen date's —
  folder. A one-shot LOCAL import (PST/mbox/maildir) keeps the folder-scoped key,
  so the same mail filed in two folders exports to both, but never twice within
  one folder. Genuine simultaneous two-folder membership observed in one run is
  recorded for that date.
- **R4 — Containment.** Every path the exporter writes stays inside the output
  root. Store/folder/attachment names derived from untrusted mail cannot
  traverse out (`..`, absolute paths, path separators, reserved device names) or
  collide destructively.
- **R5 — Crash-safe state.** The manifest and every exported `.html`/`.zip` are
  written to a temp file and renamed into place (fsynced first); an interrupted
  or failed run never corrupts the manifest, never leaves a partial exported file
  under its final name, and never records a file that was not fully written;
  stale temps and orphan zips are swept; progress already made survives.
- **R6 — Faithful structure.** Output mirrors the source folder tree; each store
  gets its own top-level directory; a single mbox/maildir folder does not
  double-nest under its own name.
- **R7 — Self-contained, legible HTML.** Each exported `.html` renders the
  message body offline: inline `cid:` images are embedded as data URIs; header
  fields are HTML-escaped. The page stands on its own for a reader with no
  tool: it links back to its folder page and the archive root, lists its
  archived attachments with a link to the sibling zip, shows Message-ID,
  Sent/Received (with their original UTC offsets), Reply-To and Bcc, and links
  the preserved original `.eml` when one was kept. Folder pages paginate (never
  truncate) and label their dates UTC; the archive root carries a README.txt
  that explains the layout without the tool.
- **R8 — Search ↔ export parity.** The index holds exactly the exported
  messages; a full-text query returns matching items; free-text is matched as
  literal terms (no FTS operator injection or query crash).
- **R9 — Read-only source.** Reading a mail store never modifies the source
  mailbox. The only exceptions are opt-in local sync/write paths that never alter
  mailbox content: `-enable-offline` edits Thunderbird's `prefs.js` (backs up
  first, refuses while the app is running), and `-outlook`'s pre-copy
  Send/Receive lets Outlook refresh its own cache. Graph capture is GET-only.
- **R10 — Robust parsing.** Malformed/truncated input (bad mbox framing,
  non-MIME message, unparseable node) is skipped or fallback-parsed — never a
  fatal crash of the whole run. Damaged input (also a damaged folder table, a
  cyclic folder hierarchy, a special file) costs the smallest unit the tool can
  isolate: the message, else the folder; some faults cost a larger unit, and
  only that unit — a failed listing of a store's root costs that store, a failed
  enumeration of Graph folders that mailbox, an unreadable PST sub-folder table
  that sub-tree. A fault that surfaces as an error or a recoverable panic never
  hangs the run.
- **R11 — Correct date semantics.** `-since` parsing is total (relative +
  absolute + rejects garbage); the date filter excludes exactly the items
  outside the window.
- **R12 — Refusal legibility.** Invalid operator input is refused with a typed
  non-zero exit and a message naming the problem — never a panic or stack trace.
- **R13 — Reindex reconciles to disk.** `reindex` reconciles the archive to what
  is on disk: every indexed message whose exported file has been deleted, moved,
  or renamed is pruned from both the search index and the manifest; surviving
  files stay searchable; the browsable folder pages regenerate from the
  reconciled set; no exported message file is deleted — only stale
  `.mailarchive-*.tmp` temps and orphan `-attachments.zip` files with no sibling
  `.html` are swept (R5, MA-69).
- **R14 — Schedule is correct, opt-in, and reversible.** `schedule` generates a
  correct scheduler entry for the host OS (cron/launchd/Task Scheduler) carrying
  exactly the operator's backup job (an export, `graph` or `reindex` job),
  validated at schedule time through the job's own flag definitions: what
  cannot run unattended (interactive prep, a non-backup verb, `-outlook` off
  Windows, a Graph job without a secret file) is refused now. It is correct for
  any host path (Windows runs the job through a batch wrapper with every token
  quoted, so the 261-character run-string limit and cmd.exe metacharacters
  cannot bite); the job writes a size-capped log; one schedule per archive by
  default (the name derives from the archive path) and an archive-local
  descriptor names it. It prints the entry by default and only applies it
  under `--install`; `--install` is idempotent (re-running yields one entry) and
  `--remove` cleanly reverses it (entry, wrapper, descriptor), leaving
  unrelated entries untouched.
- **R15 — Evolution stores read faithfully.** An Evolution store is read without
  silent loss: the local Maildir++ store's dot-encoded, `_XX`-escaped folder
  hierarchy is decoded (every subfolder read, no traversal out of the root), and
  an IMAP disk cache's per-folder maildirs are all walked, whether nested
  directly or under a `subfolders` container. Each cached message is yielded once
  with its correct folder path.
- **R16 — Outlook COM export is opt-in and safe.** On Windows with *classic*
  Outlook, `-outlook` (and the GUI's Outlook-app option) drives Outlook to write
  a fresh, standard `.pst` per mail account, which then archives through the
  normal pipeline; every created PST file name stays inside the output root. On
  any platform without classic Outlook it refuses with a legible message naming
  the requirement — never a crash. (COM behaviour is lab-validated; go-pst
  cannot read every live `.ost`, so this is the reliable path for Exchange.)
- **R17 — Graph capture is complete, read-only, incremental.** The Microsoft
  Graph source — **app-only OR delegated per-user (`-auth app|device`)** —
  enumerates every folder (including nested) — excluding the
  Deleted Items and Junk Email subtrees by default, which `-include-deleted` /
  `-include-junk` restore (the exclusion is by resolved well-known-folder id, so
  it is locale-independent — T7, operator ruling) — and every message in the
  walked folders and archives each via its raw MIME through the shared parser; it issues
  only GET requests (**app-only holds the read-only `Mail.Read` application
  permission and addresses `/users/{upn}`; delegated holds delegated read-only
  `Mail.Read` for the signed-in user's OWN mailbox and addresses `/me`** — either
  way a mailbox is never modified); and an incremental re-run archives
  zero new items and re-downloads no already-archived message body, whichever mode
  captured it (the store key is seeded from the mailbox id, not the auth mode). A cross-folder
  Internet-Message-ID hit costs only a Message-ID membership check (no body
  fetched) and downloads only a Message-ID not yet archived anywhere in the
  mailbox; an already-archived message that merely moved between folders is
  recorded as a folder change with no download (a distinct message that REUSES an
  archived id is the bounded #8 residual on this floor build — skipped without
  capture, closed by the deferred Graph ImmutableId closure). A message a run
  could not archive is recorded as a capture gap and retried by every run whose
  scope covers it.
- **R18 — Every scheduled run is recorded and `status` reports it.** A run
  writes a `running` record the moment it holds the archive lock and finalizes
  it (ok / failed / cancelled, with counts and error) on the way out; `status`
  reports the archive's completeness, last run and schedule posture as
  GREEN/WARN/RED, failing closed on missing evidence (no record, an unreadable
  record, a run that never finished, a missing or moved scheduled program, a
  scheduler that cannot be queried), every WARN/RED naming its remedy; it exits
  0 whenever it reports. The GUI's job file round-trips the wizard's answers.
- **R19 — Offline-inert archive.** Every exported `.html` is safe to open from
  disk: its `<head>` begins with a Content-Security-Policy meta identical to the
  policy `serve` sends (no scripts, no remote loads, no `<base>`, no forms) and a
  no-referrer meta, placed before any mail-supplied markup; mail-supplied
  `refresh` and `content-security-policy` metas are neutralized. `serve` sends
  that policy for archived files, a `script-src 'self'` policy for its own UI,
  HTML-escapes search snippets, never follows a symlink out of the archive root,
  and warns when bound to a non-loopback address.
- **R20 — Extract is faithful-or-absent and contained.** `extract` migrates the
  archive out as standard interchange — mboxrd (one file per folder) or
  byte-exact `.eml` (one per message, mirroring the tree) — by copying only each
  record's PRESERVED original bytes (`<stem>.eml`, kept at capture with `-raw`);
  it never synthesizes or re-serializes a message, so a record with no preserved
  bytes (every PST/OST item; any archive captured without `-raw`) is counted,
  listed and skipped, never written. Each `.eml` is confirmed through the gate
  `verify` uses — a validated relative path resolved component-wise, no symlink
  followed at any level, regular-file-only, the read bounded to the recorded
  size — and, when a `Fixity.EML` is recorded, verified against it before
  emitting; a mismatch is skipped like an absence. Output is atomic and
  idempotent (a temp file per message/folder renamed on success, truncating a
  pre-existing file never appending, so a re-run does not double) and contained
  within `-dest`, which may not equal, sit inside, or contain `-out`
  (symlink-resolved). mbox is mboxrd (a reader that unquotes `>From ` recovers
  the exact bytes); `-format eml` is byte-exact. extract writes nothing into the
  archive, holds the archive's exclusive lock for its whole run, and is not a
  schedulable backup. Its exit is `0` (whole set emitted), `3` (partial — some
  records had no preserved bytes, including an archive from which nothing is
  extractable) or `1` (refusal/error). Capture and `verify` warn when a
  raw-capable source is archived WITHOUT `-raw`, so the dependency is visible
  before the source is deleted.
- **R21 — Point-in-time truth (go-back).** The served point-in-time view shows
  exactly the messages present on disk projected to the chosen date: `serve`
  renders the current mailbox (each message under its current folder, departed
  messages hidden) and any `?at=D` (the append-only history log folded to D, each
  message under its then-folder), each intersected with the files actually on
  disk — so a message removed from the archive (its files deleted, then
  `reindex`) never appears at ANY date, whether or not the log has yet been
  compacted. The history log is append-only and reindex-compactable: `reindex`
  drops the events of a removed message so redaction spans all dates, while a
  message merely departed from the live mailbox keeps its file, its manifest row
  and its timeline (R13). The go-back page is fully server-rendered and
  script-free (R19); a hostile folder name, subject or `?at` folded into it is
  inert, escaped text and can neither inject markup nor surface an off-disk
  message. When the log is missing or damaged, `serve` announces go-back
  unavailable/partial rather than silently showing current-only, and `status`
  reports the timeline's coverage and flags a torn tail (GREEN/WARN/RED with a
  remedy, X6).
- **R29 — Copy export is separate, contained, faithful-or-absent and
  self-verifying.** An Extraction-workspace export reads Microsoft 365
  (GET-only, the signer's own mailbox) or an archive, the configured one or
  another archive folder the user chooses (read-only, never locked, never
  written) [ruling W1]. It writes ONLY inside a newly created export directory
  under the user-chosen destination, plus its own 0600 run record in the local
  state directory (XC1). It never writes into an existing export, except to
  finish this app's own interrupted run when the root's identity matches its run
  record (XC2). It never writes into the archive. Every name derived from mail
  is sanitized, contained (`os.Root`) and case-insensitively distinct. Each
  message's original is copied byte-exact or recorded absent, never
  synthesized. Derived files are labelled derived. A manifest lists every
  selected message and file with its SHA-256, and `SHA256SUMS.txt` lets a
  recipient verify with stock tools. A preview that writes nothing precedes
  every run and binds it. A run that does not finish keeps its
  `_EXPORT-NOT-FINISHED.txt` marker. One message's failure never stops the run
  and is listed with its reason.
- **R23 — Local web surfaces admit only their launching account.** `serve`,
  `setup`, the desktop dashboard and reader validate `Host` on every request and
  admit only a browser holding the per-launch session obtained through a one-time
  link the process opened or printed; refused requests get a legible page with no
  archive data; a desktop launch reaches only its own account's verified
  instance. Not a defence against a process running as the same account.
- **R25 — Source-read containment.** Reading a local store (an mbox or Thunderbird
  store, a maildir, an Evolution store) never follows a directory link (a symlink,
  or on Windows a junction or mount point), never reads a special file (a pipe,
  socket or device), and never reads a file whose link target lies outside the
  store's resolved root, unless that input's directory-link opt-in is set; then its
  directory links are followed under a visited (dev, inode) set, and file links out
  of the store and special files are still refused. It covers every file a reader
  or discovery opens, so discovery cannot hang on a pipe before the lock is taken.
  Links are judged lexically, by `readlink`, before anything is resolved. Every
  refused entry that might hold mail is recorded in the capture-gap ledger (never
  only logged, never classed info); only a proven non-mail special file is info.
  Conditions: proven on Unix. On Windows it rests on the reparse-point classifier
  (an OS-independent test over synthetic file modes, plus an unprivileged junction
  case); a cloud placeholder is read, and that case is lab-pending (MA-503). It
  assumes a static hostile store that no other local user can write: a same-user
  process racing a link swap between the check and the open, and a co-writer who
  plants a hard link, are out of scope.
- **R26 — Parse fidelity.** Every MIME leaf part and every PST attachment the
  reader meets is either archived or recorded as a parse gap, and no part's failure
  ends the parse of another.
  - *Archived:* an attachment as its transfer-decoded bytes as the source store
    holds them, never charset-converted, under its own name (a truncated part
    under `<name>.partial`); a body text part decoded to valid UTF-8 for the page
    and the index, with every inline text part kept in document order.
  - *Recorded:* a parse gap naming the part, in the manifest, the report and on
    the page, with its bytes kept as received where they exist. The gap is
    terminal where re-reading cannot change the answer, and fillable on an
    on-demand source where a later complete copy can.
- **R27 — Frozen identity, guarded repair.** A raw-MIME message's dedup identity,
  fingerprint, content digest and file stem are computed from the frozen v1
  parser's view of it, and a PST item's from its v1 attachment names. No parser
  change therefore moves an archived message's key or fingerprint. A record
  written by an older or revoked parser scheme whose message now triggers a later
  scheme's fix is re-captured through R28's commit, rewriting it at most once per
  scheme and source content; its files are those that commit gives it (its stem,
  or that stem's `-vN` successor). That happens only when:
  - the archived files are present and as recorded;
  - it is provably the same message;
  - the re-capture is never to less (R28): the source still delivers every
    archived item, matched by content, and the new capture holds at least what v1
    does.

  Otherwise the record is left as archived, its timeline is still stamped, and the
  reason is shown.
- **R28 — Re-capture is monotone.** A re-capture of an already-archived message
  never makes an item the archive holds — the body, an attachment, an inline
  image, the preserved original — empty, unreadable or absent. It deletes an
  earlier file only after no persisted manifest record or index row references
  it. Folder pages are a regenerated view, not a durable reference: they follow
  the manifest's `PagesStale` flag. Exceptions, stated here rather than elsewhere:
  - *Body:* its kind and length are not ranked; only its presence is compared.
  - *Changed size:* same-name attachments whose non-empty size changed are not
    judged; the capture commits and counts them as `changed`.
  - *Legacy-scheme adopt (DEMOTE):* a regressed legacy-scheme adopt commits, and
    its earlier file is kept on disk and owned through the new record's
    `AlsoFiles`, rather than recorded as kept.
  - *HEAL:* a re-capture whose staged bytes equal the recorded fixity may rename
    them over a missing or damaged earlier unit in place.

  A capture that would degrade is not committed (KEEP: the earlier files stay and
  nothing is written), or is merged with the held items carried forward (MERGE).
  Either way it is recorded on the record as *kept* and shown by the run summary,
  the report, `status` and `verify`. The scope is a re-capture through the shared
  exporter commit — a full run, a fill retry or a legacy re-examination, on a
  local or a live source — and so also the MIME-fidelity repair (R27).
- **R33 — One program.** Every function the product offers is a verb of one
  executable (`mailarchive`), reachable from the command line, and every schedule
  and shortcut the product installs names that executable (on Windows, the
  GUI-subsystem build `mailarchivew.exe` when it is installed beside the program
  under that name, with the same owner and the same build (§4.6.0); otherwise the
  running file, which `status` names) (SC12). The program never starts a copy of
  itself to perform a function, and no surface exists only in a file that is not
  the program. `status` tells a former name of the program from a moved or
  missing one, naming the repair. Proven through MA-610..MA-616 and MA-628;
  "never starts a copy of itself" is proven by MA-612's walk of every
  process-start site and its recording fakes (SC52). Open condition (owner
  rulings rule 3): a program path passed through a variable into an opener's
  argument is not traced by the walk (§12) (SC52). If L-W2 shows that a wrapper
  does not wait for the GUI-subsystem child, pass its stderr or pass its exit
  code, the parenthesis gains "a wrapped schedule records the console build"
  (SC22): at SE0 if L-W2 has run by then; after SE0 that is a narrowing of landed
  text, which goes to the owner under rule 2.

## 3. Scenarios

| Scenario | Must hold | Recovery/response | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 Untrusted mail names a file `../../x` or a folder `..` | R4 | name neutralized to a safe in-root segment | MA-01, MA-03, MA-29, MA-30 |
| S2 Run interrupted mid-export | R5, R2 | manifest intact; no partial html/zip visible; orphans swept; already-written items survive; resume skips them | MA-11, MA-22, MA-69, MA-94 |
| S3 Re-run over an unchanged source | R2 | zero new exports | MA-22 |
| S4 Same email filed in two folders (one-shot local import) | R3 | exported to both; never twice in one | MA-09, MA-13 |
| S5 Message with an inline `cid:` image | R7 | embedded as a data URI; renders offline | MA-19 |
| S6 Attachment/inline content not present locally | R1, R2 | skipped from the zip; recorded in the manifest (fillable or terminal) and the regenerated report; a fillable gap is re-examined by incremental runs and filled once the content is there; a legacy manifest migrates to "unknown" and is re-examined once | MA-20, MA-21, MA-66, MA-67, MA-68, MA-70, MA-71 |
| S7 Malformed or non-MIME message | R10, R1 | fallback-parsed or skipped; run continues | MA-13, MA-31 |
| S8 Search box contains FTS operators/quotes | R8 | treated as literal terms; no crash, no injection | MA-23, MA-32 |
| S9 Operator omits `-out`, gives a bad `-mode`, or serves a missing index | R12 | typed non-zero refusal naming the problem; no panic | MA-08, MA-33, MA-34 |
| S10 `-enable-offline` while the mail app is running | R9 | refused; prefs.js untouched (no backup written) | MA-27, MA-35 |
| S11 Reading a mail store | R9 | source bytes unchanged after a full read | MA-36 |
| S12 A store name/segment resolves to empty after sanitizing | R4, R6 | falls back to a stable placeholder, still in-root | MA-01 |
| S13 Operator deletes/renames exported files, then runs `reindex` | R13 | dangling index+manifest entries pruned; survivors searchable; pages regenerate | MA-40, MA-41, MA-42, MA-43, MA-44 |
| S14 Operator schedules a recurring backup, then re-runs / removes it | R14 | correct host-OS entry generated; install idempotent; remove reverses; unrelated entries kept | MA-45, MA-46, MA-47, MA-48, MA-49, MA-50 |
| S15 Evolution local Maildir++ store with nested, dot-encoded subfolders | R15, R1, R6 | every subfolder decoded and read; escaped names cannot traverse out | MA-52, MA-53, MA-55 |
| S16 Evolution IMAP disk cache: folders/<f>/{cur,new}, nested directly and via subfolders/ | R15, R1 | all folders walked; messages read as RFC822; single source | MA-54, MA-55 |
| S17 Auto-discovery hits an orphaned/corrupt Outlook `.ost` (removed account); or one locked by a running Outlook | R1 | unparseable stub excluded from the discovered set; a locked-but-valid file is kept | MA-56 |
| S18 A corrupt/truncated `.pst`/`.ost` (orphaned stub, bad OST variant) that panics go-pst | R10 | `Open` fails with a clean error; no panic escapes; the run continues and the no-console GUI never exits silently | MA-57 |
| S19 A live Exchange/IMAP `.ost` go-pst can't read; operator uses `-outlook` / the GUI Outlook-app option | R16 | on Windows, Outlook writes a clean `.pst` per account that archives normally; off Windows it refuses legibly | MA-58, MA-59, MA-60 |
| S20 Server-side archive of a (large) M365 mailbox via an app-only Graph grant, or a per-user delegated (device-code) grant against the operator's OWN mailbox | R17, R2, R9 | full first capture; incremental re-run adds nothing and re-downloads nothing; read-only; delegated mode signs in once by device code, addresses `/me`, caches a refresh token for later silent runs, archives only the signer's mailbox, and continues the SAME archive an app-only run started | MA-61, MA-62, MA-63, MA-64, MA-251, MA-252, MA-253, MA-254, MA-255, MA-256, MA-257, MA-258, MA-259, MA-260, MA-261 |
| S23 Two distinct messages share one Message-ID in one folder (a bug or a reused id) | R3, R1 | both exported and recorded (the second under a content-qualified key); an incremental re-run exports zero; names stay stable across full re-runs in any order | MA-86 |
| S24 Two messages' file stems collide (32-bit hash), or a message is re-exported under a new name | R4, R6, R13 | the second stem is lengthened deterministically from its key, never overwriting; a renamed message's old html/zip are removed | MA-87 |
| S25 A scheduled run overlaps a manual run on the same archive | R5, R12 | the second run refuses naming the lock file and the holder; nothing is written; the lock vanishes with its process | MA-85 |
| S26 Folder/subject names the file system cannot carry: illegal characters, reserved device names with extensions, trailing dots, non-Latin scripts, decomposed Unicode, very long paths | R4, R6 | names stay distinct, creatable and portable across OSes; slugs stay readable; long paths shrink the slug | MA-01, MA-03, MA-04, MA-89 |
| S27 An inheritor opens the archive folder with no tool, years later | R7 | README.txt explains the layout; every page navigates, names its attachments and links its zip/.eml; times carry their offsets; folder pages paginate | MA-90, MA-91, MA-92, MA-96, MA-13 |
| S28 Operator schedules any backup job (export, Graph, reindex) on any OS, including Windows paths with spaces/metacharacters | R14, R12 | job validated at schedule time (non-backup verbs, interactive flags, missing secret file refused); Windows wrapper quotes every token; job logs rotate; descriptor written; per-archive default name | MA-72, MA-73, MA-93, MA-97 |
| S29 Operator (or the GUI) asks whether the archive is healthy and the backup is running | R18, R12 | last-run record present and truthful; status GREEN/WARN/RED with remedies; three-state install detection; GUI job file round-trips | MA-75, MA-76, MA-77, MA-78 |
| S21 A hostile email (script, tracking pixel, remote CSS, `<base>`, meta refresh) is archived and opened from disk | R19, R7 | renders inertly: policy meta precedes the mail's markup; refresh neutralized; content preserved | MA-80 |
| S22 `serve` faces a hostile archive or network: script in a body/snippet, a symlink inside the archive, a non-loopback bind | R19, R4, R8 | UI/API/file responses carry a strict policy; snippets are escaped; symlinked paths are 404; non-loopback bind warns | MA-81, MA-82, MA-83, MA-84 |
| S30 Two mailboxes with the same store display name archived into one `-out`, or an archive upgraded from a pre-store-scoped key format | R6, R3, R2, R5, R8 | each store gets its own tree under a distinct, sticky token (the second `segment~hash`); the same mail in both stores is exported to both; an incremental re-run exports zero; an older archive re-scopes its manifest and index once, by content (from each record's own path), logged, and self-heals after an old-binary excursion without double-prefixing | MA-128, MA-129, MA-130, MA-131, MA-134 |
| S31 An archived file is bit-rotted, truncated, deleted or replaced (or predates fixity), or a manifest path is tampered, and the operator runs `verify` | R1, R4, R5 | verify re-hashes every recorded file under the archive lock and classifies each ok/modified/missing/unrecorded, reporting stray files as unexpected; a clean archive with full fixity exits 0, any modified/missing/unrecorded exits 2 (each named, `verify -record` offered for unrecorded), a refusal/error exits 1; recorded paths are validated and resolved component-wise, symlinks and non-regular files are integrity failures never followed or opened, and reads stop at recorded size+1; `-record` baselines current bytes and `status` counts coverage | MA-135, MA-136, MA-137, MA-138, MA-139, MA-140, MA-141, MA-142 |
| S32 An inheritor or auditor needs a message's original internet headers, and a hostile message forges its Received/Authentication-Results lines | R7, R19, R3 | the transport-header block is kept as stored (PST 0x007D decoded; raw sources' header section within 64 KiB) and shown in a collapsed, escaped, "unverified", 64-KiB-capped panel — never executed, never indexed, never in the fingerprint | MA-143, MA-144, MA-145 |
| S33 The search index (search.db) is lost, corrupted, or left out of a copy, but the manifest and the archived `.html`/`.eml`/`.zip` files survive; the operator runs `reindex -rebuild` | R13, R8, R5, R4 | the index and folder pages are reconstructed from the archive alone (no source, no network): a record with a preserved `.eml` is parsed from those bytes, otherwise its fields are re-derived from the archived page (searchable text, not the wire bytes); the fresh index is built into a temp db and renamed over search.db only on success, carrying no orphaned FTS row; a record whose file is missing or fails the path gate is pruned-and-reported and never read (`..`/absolute/symlinked-component/oversized skipped); a per-record read/parse/zip error is isolated, never fatal; the summary reports the from-eml vs re-derived split and the unrecovered-field count; plain `reindex` on a lost index names `reindex -rebuild`, and rebuild with no manifest refuses naming `.mailarchive-manifest.json` | MA-171, MA-172, MA-173, MA-174, MA-175, MA-176 |
| S34 Operator migrates the archive out with `extract` (mbox/eml), or points `-dest` at/inside/over `-out`, or a preserved `.eml` is symlinked/oversized/fixity-mismatched, or the archive has no preserved originals | R20, R4, R5, R12 | a `-raw` archive emits one mboxrd file per folder (`>From `-quoted, net/mail round-trips the boundaries) or a byte-exact `.eml` tree; a re-run does not double (temp+rename, truncate); a PST-only/no-raw archive emits nothing, names the skipped count and reason and exits the partial code (3); a `-dest` overlapping `-out`, a `../`/absolute/symlinked `-dest`, a symlinked/oversized `.eml` and a `Fixity.EML` mismatch are refused/skipped; extract writes nothing into the archive and is refused as a scheduled job; capture and `verify` warn when a raw-capable source is archived without `-raw` | MA-181, MA-182, MA-183, MA-184, MA-185, MA-186 |
| S35 A message carries read/importance/sensitivity state at capture (PST flags; mbox/maildir Importance/X-Priority/Sensitivity headers or the maildir "S" flag; Graph's listing `$select` for importance/read plus a best-effort PidTagSensitivity extended-property `$expand` for sensitivity — not on `$select`, MA-284/285), and later that state changes | R7, R3, R1 | the reader captures Importance/Sensitivity/Unread; the message page shows them in one HTML-escaped "Status" row when any is set (no row when none is), reversible so `reindex -rebuild` reads them back; the fields are a snapshot excluded from contentHash and Fingerprint and not indexed, so a message marked read or re-prioritised is still the same message | MA-177, MA-178, MA-179, MA-180 |
| S36 A message carries operator/source categories at capture (the PST named `Keywords` property; Thunderbird's `X-Mozilla-Keys`; Graph's `categories` array), and that classification later changes | R7, R3, R1 | the reader captures Categories (the multi-valued unicode blob parsed under hard pre-allocation bounds and a localized recover); the page shows them in their OWN HTML-escaped, control-stripped "Categories" field (never the " · " Status line), capped in number and total length, recovered per-span by `reindex -rebuild`; the fields are a snapshot excluded from contentHash and Fingerprint and not indexed, so a re-classified message is still the same message | MA-190, MA-191, MA-192, MA-193, MA-197 |
| S41 A user opens the MailArchive Desktop dashboard (`mailarchive-desktop`): a loopback-only local control panel that drives capture, sign-in, the reader, and scheduling IN-PROCESS (never a child binary), shows status and connection, and states how data is handled | R19, R17, R12 | the dashboard binds loopback only (a non-loopback bind is refused); the Overview page shows four status cards (archive engine / Microsoft 365 / capture / weekly backup) computed from real state, the Microsoft 365 connection (or a "not configured" prompt), and an OS-aware Security & privacy panel (local-only, read-only Mail.Read, sign-in in the OS vault, no third-party servers, own-mailbox-only, offline-safe pages, reversible); state-changing actions (later slices) are loopback-only + CSRF/Origin/Host-guarded; scheduled captures run the engine headless, not the dashboard ; the delegated sign-in token lives in the OS vault (Credential Manager on Windows) or the 0600 file cache elsewhere, and the dashboard shows and can clear it | MA-271, MA-272, MA-273, MA-274, MA-275, MA-276, MA-277, MA-278, MA-279, MA-280, MA-281, MA-384 |
| S40 An admin stands up a mailbox: a guided local wizard (`mailarchive setup`) collects the Entra app-registration values (tenant id, application (client) id, auth mode, and — app-only — a client secret), clarifying which id to use; the capture then runs from that config with no secret on the command line | R12, R4, R19, R17 | the wizard is a loopback-only surface (a non-loopback bind is refused) whose POST is CSRF- and anti-rebind-guarded (per-process token + loopback Origin + loopback Host) and whose secret is write-only (held for the request, written to the store, never logged or returned); non-secret config is an atomic 0600 file under the OS config dir (never inside -out), and explicit flags/env always take precedence; the client secret goes to Windows Credential Manager on Windows (lab) or a 0600 file elsewhere (plaintext at rest, same as -client-secret-file); `graph`/`schedule` fill unset tenant/client/auth from the config and resolve the app secret from the store; the field help names the Application (client) ID and warns off the two Object IDs; the reader `serve` is untouched (R19 read-only posture preserved) | MA-262, MA-263, MA-264, MA-265, MA-266, MA-267, MA-268, MA-269, MA-270 |
| S39 A v3 archive keyed by the pre-v4 folder-scoped format is opened by the live path, or two messages reuse one Message-ID across folders | R1, R2, R3, R5 | records sharing a (token, identity) with an equal, non-empty fingerprint collapse to one mailbox-wide LiveKey record (first-captured file kept, R13; each loser's kept-on-disk file recorded in AlsoFiles so a later redaction reaches every copy — collapse fabricates no history event, its pre-upgrade folder history is genuinely unknown); the collapse discriminates key shape by identity position (not NUL count) so an organic #fp-qualified LiveKey is left verbatim, and is scoped+re-keys the index atomically; distinct-fingerprint reuses stay #fp-qualified siblings (no silent drop); a pre-v6 collapsed survivor (no content hash) stays the Message-ID floor on an immutable-id-honoring walk — skipped by membership, no re-download, PhysID left unset, its AlsoFiles (redaction reach) preserved (HC2, rev-6.2); the v2→v3 re-scope is version-gated so a folder-less one-NUL live key is never mis-read as v2; the append-only history log round-trips and tolerates a torn tail (write-truncate + read-skip); Load refuses a stored version above 6 | MA-198, MA-199, MA-200, MA-201, MA-224, MA-225, MA-226, MA-240, MA-247, MA-249 |
| S38 A live-source (Graph) message moves between folders across runs, or a distinct message reuses an already-archived Message-ID in another folder | R3, R17, R1, R2, R5 | one physical copy is kept at its first-captured folder (R13); an incremental re-run records the move as a manifest folder change (Folder/LastSeen/Present) + a history folder-assertion + a body-free index folder update, with no re-download; a distinct id-reuse is split into a qualified mailbox-wide sibling so both survive (R1): on a DOWNLOAD path by content fingerprint, and on the live Graph path by the Graph IMMUTABLE ID + a recorded CONTENT HASH (a reuse whose id matches no v6 sibling is downloaded and the exporter's content-hash arbiter #fp-splits it under a PhysID-hash key — #8 CLOSED on Graph for fresh-v6 records, EC6), while a SECOND content-equal id (a reissued id, or a copy of the message in another folder) is ADDED to the record's content-equal set — one record, no duplicate, and every copy skips on later runs with no re-download or anomaly (rev-6.2 id-set); an id-withheld tenant, or a pre-v6/floor record with no content hash, stays the Message-ID-membership floor (no re-download storm, no unsafe id backfill); a full run re-materialises no per-folder duplicate; the crash order (history append+fsync → index → manifest anchor) never advances the manifest past the events that explain a move; a no-Message-ID message deduped on its content hash still stamps LastSeen and follows a move at the exporter's skip (re-downloaded each run, the acknowledged no-mid price) | MA-202, MA-203, MA-204, MA-205, MA-206, MA-244, MA-245, MA-246, MA-247, MA-248, MA-249, MA-250 |
| S37 An operator asks what a mailbox looked like on a past date, or a message is deleted online and later restored | R3, R5, R1, R2 | the append-only history log is the go-back timeline: a message absent from a FULL, lock-held mailbox walk is marked gone AFTER the walk (a {k,gone} event + manifest Present=false), its first-captured file KEPT on disk (a timeline event, not a redaction — R13/T5), scoped to the folders actually walked so a message in an excluded/unwalked folder — or any message during a partial/aborted run — is never marked gone; a re-observed message emits a present-again folder-assertion (Present false→true); the fold of the log to a date D is that day's folders/messages, which `serve` renders intersected with the files on disk (the point-in-time view, slice C) | MA-207, MA-208, MA-209 |
| S52 A user copies selected folders and messages from Microsoft 365 or the archive to a folder on disk with the Extraction workspace | R29, R4, R5, R17, R19, R20 | as R29 states: the export is written only inside a newly created export directory and never into the archive; each original is copied byte-exact or recorded absent, never synthesized; a manifest and `SHA256SUMS.txt` let a recipient verify it; one message's failure is listed with its reason and never stops the run | MA-402 (the shared Graph download, built in fault-isolation's SG slice), MA-400, MA-401, MA-403 (its slice E1's Graph reads); the workspace's other rows land with its slices E2–E6 |
| S43 A rebinding page, a hostile email link to a loopback listener, or a squatter on the dashboard port goes after a local web surface (`serve`, `setup`, the desktop dashboard or reader) | R23, R19 | a request whose Host is not `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` (any port; a non-loopback `serve` also accepts this machine's IP literals and its `-allow-host` names) is refused `403` on every route and method with no archive data; a link clicked in an archived message carries no session and no Referer; every request without the per-launch session (a path-scoped `Strict` cookie under `/s/<P>/`, obtained once through a one-time link) gets the locked page or a JSON 401, never archive data or a CSRF token; and a desktop launch reaches only this account's instance, after a challenge-response with the key in its `0600` instance file, and never opens a squatter, whatever port it holds | MA-371, MA-372, MA-373, MA-376 |
| S45 A symlink or a credential file is placed inside the archive | R19, R12, R4 | the reader serves under `/files/` only the exported pages, `.eml` files and attachment zips, and every other name and a link at any depth is 404; `graph` and `schedule` refuse a `-token-cache` or `-client-secret-file` inside `-out`, naming both paths (an unattended run warns and `status` WARNs `credential_in_archive`), and the desktop refuses an archive location that holds its settings (its scheduled capture warns and `status` WARNs `config_in_archive`); no write or delete follows a link inside the archive (a later slice) | MA-374, MA-375 |
| S46 One item or folder in a store or mailbox cannot be read, downloaded, parsed or written | R1, R10, R17, R21 | the fault is contained to that item or folder and recorded durably: an item read only in part keeps what was read as a record that names the gap (`unreadable:*`, `unparsed`); an item or folder with nothing readable is an entry in the manifest's capture-gap ledger (reason, class fillable/terminal/info, locator, source), which nothing ever consults to skip work; the ledger is bounded over the archive's life (per-token caps, an overflow entry that counts the rest exactly), bounded and control-stripped again at load, and an entry closes when its item is captured or a run that covered it no longer finds it; a degraded capture never replaces an archived record (the KEEP guard, an info `refresh-failed` row); a gap attempted on several runs escalates to WARN `capture_gaps_stuck`, never RED (the sinks land in FI-S1a; the producers land with FI-S2a, FI-S2b and FI-S3) | MA-323 (the ledger), MA-314 and MA-540 (the capture-side stops that feed it), MA-327 (its report rows); MA-310..MA-322, MA-324, MA-501 and MA-502 land with the producer slices |
| S47 A local store has a hostile or odd layout: links out of the store, FIFOs, a cyclic hierarchy | R10, R25 | a cyclic or over-deep PST folder hierarchy is walked under a visited-NID set and a depth cap, so it neither overflows the stack nor loops: a folder whose id points back at an already-walked folder is a terminal `folder-cycle` gap, one nested past the cap a terminal `folder-too-deep` gap, and every other folder is archived once (the PST half, FI-S2a); a local store's directories are held to the same cap through every descent, so a chain of thousands of nested plain directories is cut at the cap with one terminal `folder-too-deep` gap on the folder where the walk stopped, and the walk stays quick (the local half, FI-S2b). A maildir, mbox or Evolution store is read under R25: a pipe, socket or device is never opened (an info `not-a-message-file` row, so a FIFO cannot hang a run or discovery); a link to a file outside the store is a terminal `symlink-outside-store` gap and its content is never read; a directory link is a terminal `symlinked-folder-skipped` gap whose remedy names the input's directory-link opt-in (`-follow-links`), so a link loop reads each message once, not many times; with the opt-in the input keeps its token and its directory links are followed under a visited (dev, inode) set (FI-S2b) | MA-317 (PST), MA-322 (links and special files), MA-503 (a cloud placeholder on Windows, pending) |
| S48 An operator or inheritor reads the result of a partial capture | R18, R7, X1, X6 | the run summary prints a "Not captured" block (source, folder, reason, remedy; how many are new this run) and a "Recovered" line; `status` WARNs per reason with its remedy and lists up to 20 gap lines, never RED for a gap that is merely stuck; `attachments-report.tsv` carries one row per gap, with the store's source id, and is written whenever the ledger is not empty; `verify` prints an informational line and keeps its exit code; README.txt explains fillable, terminal and info; the root `index.html` carries a script-free banner (a count, never untrusted text) from every `pages.Generate` caller; a partial run exits 1 like a failed one (X1 is not amended) | MA-326 (`status`), MA-327 (report, README, banner, `verify`), MA-328 (summary); MA-325 lands with FI-S1b |
| S49 A legacy, Apple Mail, mailing-list or Outlook message is archived, and an archive written by an earlier version is upgraded. The message may have split bodies, inline named files, legacy or unknown charsets, unknown encodings, malformed parameters, cid text placeholders, a bounce sender, or an attached Outlook item | R26, R27, R1, R2, R3, R7, R8, R21 | every part is archived or recorded as a parse gap and no part's failure ends another's parse (R26); the message's key, fingerprint and content digest are those of the frozen v1 parser, so an upgrade moves no key, and an older archive's record is re-captured at most once per scheme, only while it is provably the same message and never to less (R27) | MA-330, MA-331, MA-332, MA-333, MA-334, MA-335, MA-336, MA-337, MA-338, MA-339, MA-340, MA-341, MA-342, MA-343, MA-344, MA-345, MA-346, MA-347, MA-348, MA-349 |
| S50 An archived message is re-captured while its source holds less: a full run, a fill retry or a legacy re-examination meets a same-name attachment that is now empty or unreadable, a body that is now missing, an attachment removed under the same immutable id, or a re-export without `-raw` of an archive captured with it | R28, R1, R20, R21, R17 | as R28 states: the earlier page, zip and preserved original stay as archived, byte for byte and with their recorded fixity, and the record never lists the held item as missing or fillable; the capture is recorded as kept, never as a gap, and shown by the run summary, the report, `status` and `verify`; a capture that improves one item and regresses another carries the held items forward; a legacy record whose earlier content cannot be told is left as archived and marked unverified, never claimed complete | MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355, MA-356, MA-357 (skip encoders for the confirmed-open gaps until slice RI-S5; the rows of this scenario that specify new behaviour land with the slices that build them) |
| S51 A re-capture is interrupted (a hard kill, a refused write or a full disk), or `reindex` meets an index row that names an earlier file of a message | R28, R5, R13, R8 | at every instant the persisted manifest names files that exist and match their recorded fixity; a superseded file is deleted only after the manifest that no longer names it is durable; a refused write leaves the earlier copy byte-unchanged and attested; `reindex` re-points a stale row to the record's current file and deletes none of a current record's intact files, while a message whose current and earlier files are both gone is pruned | MA-358, MA-359, MA-361 (skip encoders for the confirmed-open gaps until slices RI-S4 and RI-S2; the rows of this scenario that specify new behaviour land with the slices that build them) |
| S59 The owner meets the product through one program: a shortcut, a schedule and a terminal each start `mailarchive` (on Windows one of its pair); a no-argument launch follows §4.3 (on Linux per Q4); an upgrade from the three-file release keeps every schedule legible, and keeps it running only where the former file still exists (the MSI's copies and the `.app` link, from release N until the floor, §7.1); a schedule whose former file is gone is RED `exe_missing` with the repair its kind offers (SC41, SC37, SC39) | R33, R18, R14, R5, R12 (SC1, SC32, SC39) | a schedule naming a former name keeps running while the former file exists (§7.1, §7.2; never on Linux, §7.3) and carries a note naming the repair; when the file is gone it is RED `exe_missing` with the repair its kind offers, discoverable in `status` and every health view and not pushed; no schedule is rewritten unasked; a no-argument launch always opens a visible surface or prints the usage; no command-line verb shows a dialog, and `run-job` raises only the failure notification (MA-124, MA-624); the command line's export schedule raises none (the schedule-v2 non-goal) (SC41, SC46) | MA-610..MA-618, MA-620..MA-624, MA-628..MA-641 (MA-619 only on a Q10 yes; the lab rows MA-625..MA-627 pending); S59's Linux clause is proven by MA-617, MA-618 and MA-633, the U rows for the RED-with-repair remedy (SC41) |

Acknowledged limits (not defects): two messages that reuse one Message-ID with
an identical envelope (subject, sender, recipients, date, attachment names) and
differ only in body are treated as one message (a resend); very large attachments are buffered whole in
memory (bounded by the largest single attachment, not the mailbox); the Graph
incremental fast-path recognises an already-archived message by Message-ID
MEMBERSHIP — no envelope signature — so an archived id is skipped without download
whatever folder it is now in (R17), and a distinct message reusing that id is the
bounded #8 residual on this floor build (skipped without capture, closed by the
deferred Graph ImmutableId closure; a DOWNLOAD path — local import or full run —
splits it by content fingerprint, R1), but a message with NO
Internet-Message-ID is deduped mailbox-wide on its post-download content hash and
so is re-downloaded on every run — its identity is only knowable once the body
arrives (the acknowledged no-mid price); every manifest record
carries a 16-hex content fingerprint (~16% growth) so that Message-ID reuse is
detected for local sources; PST message categories are read from go-pst's flat
`StringToID["Keywords"]` map, so a store that also defined an unrelated named
property called `Keywords` in another property set could shadow the categories
one (an unlikely, acknowledged limit), the multi-value parse is bounds-checked
and best-effort, and real categorized-PST validation is lab-pending (MA-197).
Recorded here so a finding against them is a design conversation, not a silent
gap.

## 4. Test specs

Tiers: **U** unit property (every commit) · **S** structural whole-tree walk
(every commit) · **A** fail-closed, infra-free · **L** lab (real infra).

| ID | Tier | Asserts | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| MA-01 | U | SanitizeSegment strips separators/illegal/reserved (reserved names with any extension too), trims dots, `..`→placeholder | R4, S1, S12 |
| MA-02 | U | SanitizeSegment bounds segment length | R4 |
| MA-03 | U | SanitizeFilename preserves extension, drops path separators, never ends in a dot/space | R4, S1 |
| MA-04 | U | Slug is filesystem-safe, stable, keeps letters/digits of every script, drops bidi overrides, NFC-normalized (segments too) | R4, R6 |
| MA-05 | U | ShortHash is deterministic and collision-distinct | R3 |
| MA-06 | U | ParseSince relative windows (`30d`,`4w`,`12h`) | R11 |
| MA-07 | U | ParseSince absolute dates | R11 |
| MA-08 | U | ParseSince rejects garbage with an error (no silent zero) | R11, R12 |
| MA-09 | U | manifest Key (the one-shot LOCAL-import key) is store- and folder-scoped (same identity in a different folder, or a different store, → different keys); the live path keys per mailbox (LiveKey), not per folder | R3, R6, S4 |
| MA-10 | U | a missing manifest loads as empty, not an error | R5 |
| MA-11 | U | manifest Add/Save/reload round-trips; atomic write | R5, R2, S2 |
| MA-12 | U | decodeBytes returns UTF-8 for UTF-8 and Windows-1252 for legacy bytes | R1 |
| MA-13 | U | mbox reader extracts headers/body/Message-ID (plus Bcc, Reply-To, In-Reply-To, References, the original Date offset and the raw bytes); single file has no double-nest | R1, R3, R10, S4, S7, S27 |
| MA-14 | U | IsMailStoreDir detects mbox/maildir dirs, rejects a dir of `.pst` | R6 |
| MA-15 | U | maildir reader reads cur/new; folder = dir name | R1, R6 |
| MA-16 | S | walking a real PST fixture yields ≥1 mail item with subject | R1, R6 |
| MA-17 | U | plain body is HTML-escaped; header block + charset present | R7 |
| MA-18 | U | a full-HTML message keeps its doc and gets the metadata header injected | R7 |
| MA-19 | U | inline cid image embedded as data URI and excluded from the zip | R7, S5 |
| MA-20 | U | a zero-byte attachment is skipped and its name returned as empty | R1, S6 |
| MA-21 | U | empty-attachment and unresolved-cid produce verification issues | R1, S6 |
| MA-22 | S | full → incremental(0 new) → full lifecycle; html count matches | R2, R6, S2, S3 |

| MA-27 | U | account matched by directory; EnableOffline backs up + is idempotent | R9, S10 |

| MA-29 | U | SanitizeSegment/SanitizeFilename neutralize traversal, absolute paths, separators, reserved names | R4, S1, S12 |
| MA-30 | U | zip entry names carry no path separator or `..` (zip-slip contained) | R4, S1 |
| MA-31 | U | malformed/garbage/oversized input parses to a stub without crashing | R10, S7 |
| MA-32 | U | FTS search treats operators/quotes as literal terms (no error, no injection) | R8, S8 |
| MA-33 | U | CLI refuses missing `-out` / bad `-mode` with a typed non-zero naming the problem, no panic | R12, S9 |
| MA-34 | U | CLI `serve`/`search` with no index refuses naming the missing index | R12, S9 |
| MA-35 | U | running detected by lock-PID liveness: live lock = running; stale/dead-pid lock and persistent `.parentlock` = not running | R9, S10 |
| MA-36 | U | reading a mail store leaves its bytes unchanged | R9, S11 |
| MA-37 | U | fallback identity distinguishes messages differing only in body; Message-ID wins | R3, R1, S4 |

| MA-41 | U | reindex keeps present files searchable (survivors not dropped) | R13, S13 |
| MA-42 | U | reindex regenerates folder pages so the pruned message is no longer listed | R13, S13 |
| MA-43 | U | index EachRow enumerates rows; DeleteByID/DeleteByKey remove from docs + docs_fts | R13 |

| MA-49 | U | RemoveCronBlock reverses install by marker; empty-crontab remove is a no-op | R14, S14 |
| MA-50 | U | schedule refuses missing -out / bad -interval / install+remove with a typed non-zero naming the problem | R14, R12, S9, S14 |

| MA-648 | U | Q20 (§5.3, ruling 13.44 — refuse, NOT accepted as a residual): a schedule refuses a program an account other than the owner could replace in place, so a scheduled run can never be made to execute someone else's code as the owner. `ProgramForSchedule` is the one chokepoint (`guardLocation`); every scheduling caller (`runSchedule`, `offerSchedule`, the wizard's and the card's repair, `backupSpecFor`) then returns `ErrUnsafeLocation` with the relocation/reinstallation remedy. The Unix mechanism refuses a program directory, or any ancestor, that is world-writable WITHOUT the sticky bit (a sticky 1777 root such as `/tmp` is not flagged, so an existing file others cannot replace is safe); group-writable is deliberately NOT refused (owner ruling 2026-10-18: a normal authenticated-local-user install following the usual local-program rules — including a group-writable prefix like Homebrew's `/usr/local` or `/Applications` — is fine; Q20 refuses only a genuine other-account write). Positive twin: an owner-only directory schedules. The Windows mechanism is MA-649 | R33, S59 (SC7, Q20) |

The pure SDDL decision is unit-tested on any OS (`location_sddl_test.go`, MA-649); the live read and `ProgramForSchedule`'s `ErrUnsafeLocation` refusal are proven on real Windows by a cross-compiled test (`location_windows_test.go`, run from `\\Mac\winlab` via `prlctl exec`) that flags an `Authenticated Users: Modify` directory and clears a SYSTEM/Administrators-only one. report, `verify`, the README and the banner; `docs/design-fault-isolation.md` §9
and §10, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.4), which also lands S46
and S48. MA-323 and MA-326 span slices: each carries a skip encoder, citing its
row, for the cases a later slice builds (the legacy and parser-generation cases,
the `-since` rule of a Graph walk, the partial flag, the desktop view). MA-329's
pre-change golden was taken before any S1a code.
MA-315, MA-316, MA-317, MA-318, MA-319, MA-320, MA-324 and MA-504 are fault
isolation's slice S2a (PST isolation and healing: the reader's own row and
sub-folder loops, store-uid locators, the local run's classification of export
errors, recovery and closure of gaps, the `-outlook` export-skip gaps, the
legacy-placeholder re-class; `docs/design-fault-isolation.md` §4.4, §4.6, §9 and
§10, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.4), which also lands S47 and
R10's added sentences, and MA-323's legacy-placeholder case. MA-316 and MA-318
span slices: each carries a skip encoder, citing its row, for the cases a later
slice builds (the Graph, maildir, mbox and Evolution folder cases; the raw-stub
cases and the Graph twin). MA-504 is tier L and pending, its lab story a Windows
host with classic Outlook; the mapping it checks on a real host is unit-tested
(MA-320). N9's corrupted-`MessageCount` case is a skip encoder citing R1.
MA-321, MA-322 and MA-503 are fault isolation's slice S2b (local containment: the
mbox, maildir and Evolution readers' per-file and per-folder isolation, containment
at every local open, the lexical link judgment through `os.Root`, the Windows
reparse classifier, `util.OpenRegularFile`, the per-input directory-link opt-in,
and the raw-preserving stubs with their parser generation;
`docs/design-fault-isolation.md` §4.5, §9 and §10, ids from
`docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.4), which also lands R25 and the links and
special-file half of S47, and closes the maildir, mbox and Evolution cases of
MA-316, the raw-stub cases of MA-318 and the parser-generation case of MA-323.
MA-503 is tier L and pending, its lab story a Windows VM with Files-On-Demand; the
classifier it checks on a real host is unit-tested (MA-322). The collapse of two
distinct unparseable raw messages in one folder (the identity fold) waits for the
owner's answer to ESC-1 and stays a skip encoder citing R1 in MA-318's raw-stub
case.
MA-372, MA-373, MA-376, MA-377 and MA-384 are local confidentiality's slice C2
(sessions and `/s/<P>/`, link delivery for `serve`, `setup` and the desktop, the
per-account desktop instance, the security copy; `docs/design-local-confidentiality.md`
§4.C2–C4 and §9, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.4), which also lands
X12 in `docs/ux-contract.md`. MA-377 is tier L and pending: its lab story is the
two-account Windows 11 drill (and Safari on macOS) the row states. MA-384's
extraction-workspace sign-in surface is slice E3's, so its case is a skip encoder
citing MA-384 until E3 builds the surface. The Covers cells cite S42, which lands
with slice A (deferred, rulings §13.10); no covers marker cites it until it exists.

MA-330..MA-349 are MIME fidelity's slice S0 (`docs/design-mime-fidelity.md` §9
and §10, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.3 and §8.4), which also
lands R26, R27 (the J6 text, with no file promise for a repair) and S49. S0 builds
no parser change: each U row is a skip encoder citing its row until the slice that
turns it green (S1: MA-330, MA-331, MA-347's identity half, MA-346 (a, c); S2:
MA-332, MA-333 (non-page), MA-334, MA-337, MA-338, MA-340 (bodies), MA-349; S3:
MA-335, MA-336, MA-340, MA-341; S4: MA-339, MA-333 (page), MA-348; S5: MA-342; S6:
MA-343; S7: MA-344, MA-345, MA-346 (b, d), MA-347 (repair half)). MA-343 is tier L
and pending: its lab story is the Outlook-on-Windows export the row states, and
S6 may not merge while it is L. Each row's Prove-fail mutation is folded into the
end of its Asserts cell, because this table has four columns. R27 cites R28, which
lands with re-capture integrity's slice S1. The fidelity corpus (`testdata/mime/`,
indexed by `corpus.tsv`, with the PST twin `testdata/support.pst`) and the identity
and content goldens recorded over it from the release build v0.7.1 are in place, with
their provenance in the goldens' headers; `TestMimeCorpusIsComplete` and
`TestMimeGoldensHoldForTheBuild` (citing R27) guard them until S1 points them at the
frozen replay. No corpus input panics the v1 parse, so MA-330's panicking-item case
stays open as a skip, and the identity golden pins the stub from a constructed
message. The design's R2 clarification (§6) does
not land here (ruling J7): it waits for ESC-2, as one joint paragraph with
re-capture integrity's R2 amendment.
Slice MF-S1 closes MA-347's identity half with every write recording `chash` (MC5)
and `parse_scheme` (MC12); MA-329's baseline `internal/app/testdata/p14-golden.json`
was regenerated with those two additive fields on the healthy records by the owner's
decision of 2026-10-11, and nothing else in it moved.
Slice MF-S2 (`docs/design-mime-fidelity.md` §10, rulings §9 row 20) lands the fidelity
walker, its parameter, text and transfer decoding, the gap classes and bounds (P2, P4
`.partial`, P5, P10, P13), the shared `internal/util` primitives (MC35), the report
kinds, counts and wording, and `BenchmarkParseCorpus` (MC39). It adds no row and no
R, S or X id: the rows are the S0 rows above. It turns MA-332, MA-333 (non-page),
MA-334, MA-337, MA-338, MA-340 (bodies) and MA-349 (parser bounds) into real tests and
MA-348's counts into a real test. What each of those rows leaves for a later slice is a
skip encoder citing the row: MA-333's page assertion (MF-S4), MA-340's headers (MF-S3),
MA-348's page blocks and repair figures (MF-S4, MF-S7) and MA-349's export-side recovers
(MF-S4, MF-S7). Identity stays the frozen replay's: no identity or content golden moves.
The walker is ON (owner-approved 2026-10-12, rulings §13.29): `fidelityContent` in
`internal/source/mimeroute.go` is true and `state.ParseSchemeCurrent` is 2 (the S2 notes
carry `state.ParseSchemeFidelity`). Switching on narrowed four assertions of MF-S1's tests
(`TestFidelityParserKeepsTheIdentityGoldenOnTheCorpus`'s "content from v1", "v1 panics"
and "the content parser panics" subtests, and `TestMixedVersionsKeepIdentityAndStems`)
and moved `parse_scheme` from 1 to 2 on the 46 records of MA-329's baseline, nothing
else; no identity golden moved. A message v1 panics on keeps the stub identity (its terms
carry `NoIdentityDate`; `TestAStubIdentityMessageKeepsItsDisplayDatesAndTheStubIdentity`).
A parse gap is a message-level `ParseGap` or an attachment's `GapKind`, filed as a
label in the record's own Missing or Terminal list (not as a `ReadGaps` label, whose KEEP
guard would hold back every re-read of such a record). The README documents the new
report kinds and `status -json` keys; the friction review and X-series check of the new
wording are owed (design §10).

MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355, MA-356, MA-357, MA-358, MA-359 and MA-361 are
re-capture integrity's slice S1, interim (`docs/design-recapture-integrity.md` §9
and §10, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.3, §8.4 and §13.1), which
also lands R28, S50 and S51 and closes R27's reference to R28. S1 builds no product
code and lands only the rows of the confirmed-open gaps, each a skip encoder citing
the existing invariant its defect breaks: F46 and the legacy re-examination
(MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355) and the immutable-id placement of §1.6 (MA-356)
cite R1, the preserved original of §1.5 (MA-357) cites R20, N2, N3 and the
unchanged-path window of §1.7 (MA-358, MA-359) cite R5, and F48 (MA-361) cites R13.
Each encoder builds its archive with the real pipeline and runs a healthy twin first
(green today), then asserts what the row requires of today's surface, so it goes
green when the gap is fixed and, with its skip removed, fails today for the reason
its skip message names; the kept, unverified and wording assertions of a row (`Kept`,
`Stats.Kept`, the report and summary lines) and the fault seams it names (the kill
points C1..C8, the injected ENOSPC, the probe's temp counter) are added by the slice
that builds them; so do the cases that need the inventory or the verdicts themselves
(the client-placeholder part, the frozen-fingerprint label drift, the relabelled item
matched by CRC, a refused earlier original, the DEMOTE half and the timeline stamps of
MA-356, case (c) of MA-357 and its carried pre-fixity original). The crash cases
(MA-358) observe the archive from inside the run, after a message is committed and
before the next checkpoint save, which is the state a hard kill there leaves, and
assert that witness first: the snapshot holds the stale lock file and the manifest
the run started from, and, with the index kept, none of the run's index batch. The
unchanged-path result (§1.7) goes with ESC-3.
The slices that turn them green: RI-S5 (MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355, MA-356, MA-357), RI-S4
(MA-358, MA-359; MA-358's same-path case waits on ESC-3) and RI-S2 (MA-361). MA-361
spans two slices: its `-vN` redaction and N2-adoption twins are skip encoders that
RI-S2 lands (ESC-2). The rows that specify new behaviour (MA-353, MA-354, MA-360,
MA-362, MA-363, MA-364) land with the slice that builds them (RC32). No existing
catalog text changes here: the R2, R13 and S24 amendments wait for ESC-2, R1's
added sentence lands with RI-S5, R5's with RI-S4 and only on ESC-3 (a), and S39 is
unchanged (J2). R27's reference to R28 now resolves.
